| Chapter 3: reading and writing decimal fractions — THIRD CHAPTER (Гуравдугаар бүлэг) — reading and writing decimal fractions (Аравтын бутархайг унших, бичих): can you read the number measures in the pictures? Discuss (the calculator's display, the juice bottle marked 1.5 l, the 2.5 m road sign, the thermometer showing 36.4°); observe in what form the common fraction is written and discuss (the whole square marked "1 (one whole)"; the strip divided into 10 with 3 parts shaded: 3/10 = 0.3; the 100-grid with 47 cells shaded: 47/100 = □.□□; the 100-grid half shaded: 50/100 = □.□□; a boy's note: "The fraction 3/10 is written as 0.3, and the fraction 47/100 is written as 0.47"; a girl's rule: "A common fraction with the denominator 10 or 100, written using a point (цэг), is called a decimal fraction (аравтын бутархай)"; the place diagram of 0.47 — the whole part (Бүхэл) and the fraction part (Бутархай) separated by the point, with the places named: the whole part's places and the fraction part's tenths place (Аравны орон) and hundredths place (Зууны орон); the writing rule: "When writing: write the whole part, put a point (.) after it, and write the fraction part. The point that separates the whole part and the fraction part is called the whole point (decimal point). When reading: beginning from the whole part, 0.47 is read 'zero whole forty-seven hundredths'"); write the ribbon's length first as a common fraction with a denominator and then discuss with what number it is written (the ruler from 0 to beyond 3 cm with its millimetre marks, and the orange and blue ribbons measured against it, each to be written as □□/□□ cm = □.□ cm); read the decimal fractions written in the place-class table and write them in your notebook (the table with the whole part's places — the hundreds place (Зуутын орон), the tens place (Аравтын орон), the ones place (Нэгжийн орон) — the point, and the fraction part's places — the tenths place, the hundredths place and the thousandths place (Мянганы орон): the rows give 5.3; 4.25, read "four whole twenty-five hundredths"; 38.63; 120.74; 267.08, with their writings and readings to complete); place the numbers in the place-class table and read them (a. five whole twenty-five hundredths (5.25); b. forty-eight whole six hundredths (48.06); c. three hundred forty-six whole nine tenths (346.9); d. two thousand seven whole seven hundredths (2007.07)); read the number that corresponds to the dot marked on the number line (the line from 0 to 1 divided in tenths, with dots at the tenths positions); write the decimal fractions (the place pictures: the ones-place blocks, the point, the tenths bars and the hundredths dots, making 2.6□ and 1.43); an activity: two pupils play in turn using the number place-value cards (a. the first pupil says a decimal fraction; b. the next pupil builds that fraction number with the number place-value cards; c. if it is built correctly, he scores a point; d. swap and play; e. the one with the most points wins; the example: 5.34 is built from the cards "5 — five whole", ".3 — three tenths" and "04 — four hundredths") | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 97–98 |
| Decimal and common fraction conversions; decimals on the number line — write the common fractions as decimal fractions (2 9/10; 3 57/100; 8/10; 8 6/10; 7 68/100; 1/100); write the decimal fractions as common fractions (2.8; 25.3; 7.84; 0.06; 2.60; 37.41); read the decimal fraction and write it (a. three whole eleven hundredths (3.11); b. zero whole eight hundredths (0.08); c. thirty-two whole three hundredths (32.03)); read the decimal fractions and write them in words (8.7; 0.37; 0.06; 12.01; 100.1); place the decimal fractions in the place-class table and write what place the picked-out digit expresses (1.75; 0.57; 5.32; 2.45; 51.1; 0.35); write the suitable number and read it (13/100 = □; 7/10 = □; 48/100 = □; 0.04 = □/□; 0.16 = □/□); express the shapes' shaded and unshaded parts as decimal fractions and read them (the two strips divided into 10 with some rows shaded, and the 100-grid with about half its cells shaded); mark the fractions 0.3, 0.8, 0.5 and 0.90 on the number line with dots (the line from 0 to 1 in tenths — 0.90 lands on the same point as 0.9); an activity: each pupil prepares a square divided into 10 equal parts (a. colour 5 parts of the square divided into 10 equal parts in green; b. colour 50 parts of the square divided into 100 equal parts; c. express the coloured parts as common fractions and write them; d. write your common fraction as a decimal fraction, draw a conclusion and discuss — the two colourings are equal, 0.5 = 0.50); discuss how the liquid measures were expressed as decimal fractions (the groups of 1-litre vessels whose combined amounts are written as 2.9 l and 3.4 l); express the liquid measures as decimal fractions and write them (the four groups of vessels with their levels); express the insects' length measures in centimetres and read them (the ant (Шоргоолж): 8/10 cm = 0.8 cm, worked; the dragonfly (Соно): □/□ cm = … cm, measured against the pictured ruler); express the measure in the indicated unit (7 mm = □ cm; 5 cm 8 mm = □ cm; 10.8 cm = □ cm □ mm; 900 ml = □ l; 7 l 500 ml = □ l; 9.3 l = □ l □ ml); complete the number line's scale and write it (the line from 0 to 0.1 divided in hundredths, with 0.01 marked); join each number to the corresponding scale mark of the number line (the numbers 3.48, 3.52, 3.43, 3.40, 3.50, 3.59 against the line from 3.4 to 3.6); find the fractions in which the zero cannot be dropped (0.05; 1.04; 5.80; 125.05; 75.60; 0.01; the rule box: "A zero at the very end position of the fraction part's digits can be dropped. But the zeros of the fractions 0.05 and 1.008 cannot be dropped") | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 99–100 |
| Division giving decimal quotients — let's discuss the quotient's value (Ногдворын утгыг ярилцаарай): a boy poured 3 litres of fruit juice equally into four glasses: how many litres of juice were poured into one glass (the long division worked in the grid: 3.00 ÷ 4 = 0.75 (l), through 28, 20, 20, 0); discuss what action is carried out when an improper fraction is turned into a decimal fraction (the division of 39 by 10 worked in the grid with its remainder 9, and the flow: improper fraction (Засагдах бутархай) → mixed fraction (Холимог бутархай) → decimal fraction (Аравтын бутархай): 39/10 = 3 9/10 = 3.9; then 39/10 = □.□ to write directly); when carrying out the division action, express the quotient as a decimal fraction (the worked grids: 2 ÷ 4 = 0.5; 2 ÷ 5 = 0.4; 32 ÷ 10 = 3.2; 157 ÷ 100 = 1.57); draw a segment 2 dm long in your notebook and divide it into four equal parts: how many decimetres long did one part become (0.5 dm)?; if 4 litres of fruit drink are poured equally into five vessels, how many litres of drink are in each (0.8 l)?; 5 presents were wrapped with a 4 m ribbon: with how many metres of ribbon was one present wrapped (0.8 m)?; carry out the action on the number machine, replacing the common fraction's sign with the division sign (the pictured calculator, and the shapes standing for 1/4, 2/5, 1/2 and 3/4): discuss the quotient's value being expressed as a decimal fraction; find the pattern and write the next 3 numbers (a. 1/2, 2/6, 4/18, 8/54, □, □, □ — the numerator doubling and the denominator tripling; b. 2/3, 6/9, 18/27, 54/81, □, □, □ — both tripling); an activity: express the given numbers by dividing the numerator by the denominator, and the quotients' values as decimal fractions: use the number machine (the example: 1/4 = 1 ÷ 4 = 0.25; then 2/5; 8/10; 8/5; 15/4; 6/8 — including the improper fractions 8/5 = 1.6 and 15/4 = 3.75) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 101 |
| Comparing and rounding decimal fractions — comparing decimal fractions (Аравтын бутархайг жиших): in the long-jump competition a boy jumped 1.58 m and a girl jumped 1.56 m: which of the boy and the girl jumped the longer distance (Method 1: compare by the position on the number line — a girl's note: "1.58 is located on the right-hand side of 1.56 on the number line, so 1.56 < 1.58"; Method 2: compare by place value — the flow boxes: compare the whole parts (1 = 1), then compare the tenths parts (5 = 5), then compare the hundredths parts (8 > 6): the conclusion 1.58 > 1.56); mark the numbers on the number line with dots and compare them (a. 0.9; 0.4; 0.6; 0.8; 0.5; b. 2.3; 2.7; 2.9; 3.1; 3.4); compare the fractions (0.38 □ 0.29; 1.8 □ 1.80; 8.27 □ 8.29; 5 □ 4.99); compare the measures (2 tögrög 97 möngö □ 2 tögrög 79 möngö; 5 m 3 dm □ 5 m 3 cm; 117 l 03 ml □ 112 l 95 ml; 3 kg 540 g □ 3 kg 450 g); the box: "Writing more than two fractions in a row from the biggest to the smallest is called writing in decreasing order; writing them from the smallest to the biggest is called writing in ascending order"; write the fractions in decreasing order (0.7; 0.9; 0.23; 1.5; 0.74; and 1.6; 0.8; 2.4; 1.66; 1.70); what place does the digit 4 indicate in the fraction 1.24?; 1 cm is equal to what part of 1 m (0.01)?; compare (5 km 250 m … 5 km 30 m; 7 l 250 ml … 7 l 205 ml); a self-check: place the fractions in decreasing order (0.80; 12.0; 44.3; and 1.01; 1.4; 1.43; and 0.54; 0.45; 0.5); rounding a decimal fraction to a whole (Аравтын бутархайг бүхлээр тоймлох): say approximately how many metres the ribbon is: round using your knowledge of rounding natural numbers by the two methods, applied to the decimal fraction (a boy's note: "The ribbon's length is approximately 5 metres"; Method 1: round by the position on the number line — 4.8 m ≈ 5 m on the line from 0 to 6; Method 2: round by comparing the digit after the rounding place with 5 — the steps: Step 1: determine the rounding place's digit; Step 2: compare the digit after the rounding place with 5; a girl's rule: "If the digit after the rounding place is 5 or greater than 5, the rounding place is increased by 1" — worked both to a whole number and to tenths (5.48 ≈ 5.5)); check whether the roundings are correct and discuss them (the four worked roundings of 7.61, 1.39 and 10.82 to wholes and to tenths, some of them wrong as printed, to be judged); round to a whole (the table: the numbers 2.57, 0.35, 17.49, 0.77, 10.61, 1.8, 100.32 with the whole-number row begun: 3, 0, …); write the nearest whole numbers that fit the inequality (□ < 4.4 < □; □ > 7.38 > □; □ > 18.47 > □); a. what fraction do the shaded parts express — write them and compare them (the two 100-grids); b. round the decimal fractions to wholes; c. colour the part expressed by the fraction 0.8; write the fractions that round to one whole; round the decimal fractions to wholes (3.2; 14.3; 17.1; 17.5; 39.47; 39.51) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 102–103 |
| Test your own skill (Өөрийн чадвараа сориорой): say which picture's number expresses the fraction 0.8 (the circle divided into sectors, and the two grids divided into 10 parts with parts shaded); round the fractions to wholes (3.18; 0.76; 0.49; 2.98; 0.05; 5.55); write the fractions 1.23, 0.89, 2.1, 0.98 in ascending order; write the equal fractions among 1.5; 1.05; 1.50; 0.7; 0.07; 1.500; depict the fractions 8/10 and 9/10 in a picture; can the fraction 6/4 be rounded to a whole? Explain; say the number closest to 5341 (a. 5000; b. 5300; c. 5390; d. 5400 — rounding to different places); write all the possibilities for the digits that make the inequality true (0.57 > 0.5□; 0.4□ < 0.42; 0.□3 > 0.83; 0.46 < 0.□6); a boy thought of an even number: when he rounded that thought number to hundreds, 200 came out: find all the possibilities of the number the boy could have thought of (the even numbers from 150 to 249); in the football cup competition the teams scored as follows: the winning team got 3 points, a drawn game 1 point, the losing team 0 points: Team A got 11 points: playing the fewest games, in how many games could Team A have got this score (5 games: 3 + 3 + 3 + 1 + 1)?; round by the whole measure (a. the lengths 4.6 cm, 12.1 cm, 5.4 km, 10.8 km, 6.3 mm, 0.6 dm; b. the weights 3.7 kg, 2.9 kg, 8.1 kg, 26.3 g, 9.8 g, 0.9 kg; c. the times 3 hours, 18 minutes, 5 hours 47 minutes, 55 minutes, 1 hour 30 minutes; d. the liquids 3.2 l, 4.8 l, 32.4 ml, 5.6 l, 14.6 ml — each rounded to its nearest whole unit); a puzzle: in how many ways can the following words be read (the letter grids in which the word БАГШ (teacher) and another word are traced along the letters) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 104 |
| Adding decimal fractions: the column method — adding and subtracting decimal fractions (Аравтын бутархайг нэмэх, хасах): observe how a decimal fraction is added and discuss (the place-value model: the whole part's unit blocks and the tenths part's bars for 1.5 + 0.6 — five bars and six bars make eleven bars, one whole and one bar, giving 2.1; a boy's rule: "A decimal fraction is added by placing the places opposite each other, aligned at the whole's point (the decimal point). The whole parts are added on the whole part"; the column examples begun: 5.2 + 7.3; 2.5 + 3.4; 8.9 + 8.5); observe how a decimal fraction is added (the bigger place chart of tens, ones and tenths for 8.9 + 4.5 = 13.4, with the carries 1, 1 shown in the column); add the decimal fractions (the column sets with their carries marked: 2.5 + 18.9 = 21.4; 12.5 + 8.9; 37.5 + 21.9; 97.5 + 21.3); write each number as the sum of its whole and fraction parts (12.7 = 12 + 0.7; 23.5 = □ + □; 41.5 = □ + □; 5.41 = □ + □); find the sums' values (4.7 + 6.8; 14.7 + 26.8; 32.8 + 1.5; 133.5 + 25.7); a boy's rule: "When adding decimal fractions, the corresponding places are written in one column and added like the addition of a many-digit number"; find the sums mentally (2.5 + 7.5; 7.5 + 2.4; 12.8 + 0.2; 9.4 + 0.6); two pencils are placed joined together: find what measure they make, and explain how you added (the two pencils along the ruler: 8 5/10 cm + 7 3/10 cm = 15 8/10 cm, shown three ways: in millimetres (85 mm + 73 mm = 158 mm), in centimetres and millimetres (8 cm 5 mm + 7 cm 3 mm = 15 cm 8 mm), and in decimal centimetres (8.5 cm + 7.3 cm = 15.8 cm)); find the sums' values (the column additions: 17.5 + 25.4; 75.8 + 13.2; 8.35 + 15.21; 12.5 + 15.2; 15.8 + 23.2; 4.45 + 12.61); using the addition action's place-swapping property, calculate mentally (4.8 + 0.9 + 3.2; 0.1 + 0.3 + 0.9; 0.8 + 0.3 + 5.2 + 2.7); find the frame length of a rectangular vegetable field 27.5 m long and 12.7 m wide (the perimeter, 80.4 m); a company sowed crops on three fields: the I field is 360.8 ha, the II field is 24.7 ha greater than the I field, and the III field is 45.9 ha greater than the II field: on how many hectares of field did the company sow crops in all (1177.7 ha); race your friend to find the sums (the brick pyramids of decimals: the piles built on 3.6; 3.6; 3.6 and on 5.3; 7.2; 9.1, each row to be added); the rule box: "When adding a whole number and a fraction number, the whole is added to the whole and the fraction part is added in its place, and by writing so, the numbers of digits can be made equal"; correct the following expressions' mistakes and find the sums' values (the five column additions whose decimal points have been written out of alignment — 3.12 with 6.00; 68.23 with 7.50; 12.62 with 9.10; 3.62 with 5.1; 8.20 with 9.56 — to be rewritten with the points aligned); a puzzle: if the whole numbers from 1 to 100 are written one after another in a row, a number of how many digits is formed (192 digits) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 105–106 |
| Adding and subtracting on the number line; decimal problems — carry out the actions using the number line (a. the point A is at 7.5: the point B lies 3 units to the right of A: write the position of the point B — the sum of 7.5 and 3 is □ (10.5); b. the point B lies 3 units to the left of A: the difference of 7.5 and 3 is □ (4.5); c. from A move 3 units to the left to reach the point B, and from there move 6 units to the right to reach the point C: write C's position (from 8.5: 8.5 − 3 + 6 = 11.5); d. two flags stand 5 units apart between the marks 2 and 8: write all the possibilities for where they stand, expressed in tenths (for example 2.4 and 7.4); e. the point A stands on the mark 7 and the point B on the mark 7.6: count the decimal fractions standing between them and write them (7 < □ < 7.6)); compose problems from the pictures and solve them (a. the bar model: the whole 4.2 km with the parts 1.7 km and ?; b. the bar models totalling 12.5 l); a self-check: 1. write the expressions and find their values (a. find the gap between 3.8 and 4.8; b. depict the whole-unit segment between 5.2 and 9.4; c. a cricket that can jump one unit at a jump: how many jumps from 2.5 to 8.5 (6 jumps)?); 2. find the sums' values (125.13 + 18.72; 75.05 + 29.1; 901.07 + 35.69); a puzzle: on each page, the letters A, B, C and the numbers 1, 2, 3 are written: that letter and that number are paired: in how many ways can the letter-number pairs be formed (9 ways); observe how the number place-value cards are used and explain (the place chart of the whole part — hundreds (Зуу), tens (Арав), ones (Нэгж) — the point, and the fraction part — tenths, hundredths, thousandths: numbers composed by overlapping the place cards, and the addition 5.04 + 2.08 = 7.12 shown with the card overlays); write the suitable number (72.15 = □ whole and □ hundredths; 18.20 = □ whole and □ tenths; 5.28 = □ whole and □ hundredths; 18.86 = □ whole and □ hundredths); what number do the highlighted cards make? Read the other number formed (the card grid of the hundreds 100-900, the tens 10-90, the ones 1-9, the tenths 0.1-0.9 and the hundredths 0.01-0.09, with the cards 200, 20, 5, 0.4 and 0.04 picked out, making 225.44); look at the picture of the distances between the neighbouring households, compose problems and solve them (the route A–Б 11.1 km, Б–В 13.5 km, В–Г 9.7 km, Г–Д 8.6 km — the whole route A to Д is 42.9 km); an activity: the game board from ГАРАА (the start) with its grid of place values 10, 1, 0.1, 0.01: 1. a boy reached a girl with 47.65 points: which members (cells) did the boy pass over?; 2. if the boy first had 10 points, which member did he pass over to reach the girl with 45.88 points?; 3. compose a similar problem about the girl reaching the boy | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 107–108 |
| Subtracting decimal fractions: borrowing; problems — observe how decimal fractions are subtracted and discuss (the place-value model: the whole part's blocks with strokes through them and the tenths bars crossed out, for 3.5 − 2.3 = 1.2; a girl's column rule: "A decimal fraction is subtracted by placing the places opposite each other, aligned at the whole's point"); find the differences' values (17.5 − 1.3 = 16.2, worked; 48.9 − 3.8; 78.9 − 6.7; 198.8 − 27.5); find the difference's value (the place chart of 2.4 − 0.6 = 1.8 with the borrowing shown; a boy's note: "When subtracting from the tenths part, one whole of the whole part is broken up and transferred to the tenths part to subtract. It must not be forgotten to write the numbers with their whole points aligned opposite each other"; the items: 72.3 − 6.7; 17.4 − 1.5); find the difference's value (the hundredths place chart: 3.42 − 0.25 = 3.17); find the differences' values (1.25 − 1.13; 1.25 − 0.17; 1.25 − 0.39; 97.5 − 28.3); find the differences mentally (12.6 − 5.6; 13.7 − 2.7; 13.9 − 4.9; 10.3 − 4.3); by what methods can the difference of two pencils' length measures be found (measure your own pencils, find the difference and express the measure in centimetres: the worked example 14.5 − 9.8 in two forms — 145 mm − 98 mm = 47 mm, and 14.5 cm − 9.8 cm = 4.7 cm — with the check (Шалгая): 9.8 + 4.7 = 14.5 cm); find the differences' values (75.38 − 47.38; 438.38 − 94.28; 348.67 − 65.08; 24.36 − 8.43; 843.48 − 539.76); find the expressions' values (7.6 − 5.6 + 3.2; 13.7 + 6.3 − 2.5; 13.9 − 4.8 − 1.7); a girl's rule box: "When a decimal fraction is subtracted from a whole number, a point is put after the minuend, the fraction part's places are completed with zeros, and the action is carried out" (the worked set of whole-minus-decimal subtractions, written as 8.00 − 0.84 and the like); to pack it in 5 kg, how many kilograms of flour must be added (the three pictured scales with their piles of flour — read each scale and find the complement to 5 kg); a traveller travelled 27.5 km on the first day and 2.8 km less on the second day than on the first day: how many kilometres did the traveller travel in 2 days (52.2 km); an isosceles triangle's perimeter is 14.8 cm and its side is 5 cm: find the length of the triangle's base (4.8 cm); find the expressions' values ((18.6 − 0.9) + (3.78 − 1.09) + 10.1; (75.43 − 29.81) + 36.45 − (1.09 + 0.01)); find the letter expressions' values (if a = 4.7: 27.5 − (15.3 + a); and 27.5 − 15.3 + a) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 109–110 |
| Sum-and-difference tables; how a sum changes — observe the tables and find the sums' and differences' values (the addition table: the rows 1, 10, 100 against 85.1, 31.5, 10.9, with 1 + 85.1 = 86.1 given; the subtraction table: the rows 1, 10, 100 against 185.1, 221.9, 100.9, with 185.1 − 1 = 184.1 and 221.9 − 10 = 211.9 given); make an experiment by writing sum expressions, and complete the following conclusions in your notebook (a. if one addend is increased by 3.6, the sum's value … by □ …; b. if each addend is increased by 1.5, the sum increases by □ …; c. if one addend is increased by 1.5 and the other is decreased by 1.5, the sum's value … — prove your answer by comparing with an example); a warehouse held some grain: on the first day 6.3 centners of grain were milled, on the second day less than that, and on the third day less than on the first two days: how many centners of grain were milled in the warehouse in all (a three-day milling problem with decimal differences); travellers decided to travel along a nature-reserve route: on the first day they travelled 353.7 km, on the second day 113.4 km more than on the first day, and on the third day 389.8 km: how many kilometres did they travel in all (1210.6 km); an activity: let's play at making fraction numbers (a. two children in turn toss 2 dice: from the fallen spots' count, compose a decimal fraction and write it in your notebook; b. who has composed the bigger number? Compare them: say the sum of your two composed numbers mentally; c. find the difference); write in your notebook the numbers that fit in place of the question marks (the five number triangles with their decimal entries — 7.5 with 1.13 and 2.5; 8.2 with 4.5; 17.5 with 3.4; 20 with 9.5; 15 with 3.4 — the missing corner to find from the others); continue the number sequences (a. the chain increasing by 0.2: ?, 0.6, 0.8, ?, ?; b. the chain decreasing by 0.5: ?, ?, ?, 4, ?) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 111 |
| Mental decimal arithmetic; the bridge problem; decimal magic squares — write in your notebook the number that makes the inequality true (37.86 < 37.□6; 0.3□ < 0.37; 84.□□ > 84.94; 129.04 > 129.0□; 4.5□ < 4.57; 37.076 > □8.972); calculate mentally (the complement pairs to 1: 0.4 and 0.6; 0.2 and 0.8; 0.5 and 0.5; 0.7 and 0.3; 0.1 and 0.9; and the complement pairs to 10: 1.3 and 8.7; 3.3 and 6.7; 4.4 and 5.6; 6.6 and 3.4 — each pair to be completed); calculate the expressions mentally by the easy method (1.2 + 0.7 + 0.8 + 2.3; 0.5 + 2 + 16 + 0.5 + 0.4; 1.1 + 0.4 + 3.2 + 0.9 + 0.4; 6.5 + 0.1 + 3.5 + 0.9 — grouping the pairs that make whole numbers); the bridge's load capacity is 15 kg: which animals can cross the bridge together (the pictured animals: the monkey (Сармагчин) 9.4 kg, the squirrel (хэрэм) 2.1 kg and the rabbit (туулай) 5.8 kg: a. can the rabbit cross the bridge together with the squirrel (7.9 kg — yes)?; b. can the rabbit cross together with the monkey (15.2 kg — no, it exceeds the capacity)? Why?); observe the flow diagrams and carry out the actions (a. the yes/no machine: 12.7 enters, a number is added, the result is compared with 10, and the loop branches by − 3.5 and + 5.3; b. the chains of unknowns with the steps − 1.3 to 8, + 4.9 to 3, + 0.5 to 8 and + 1.7 to 4); a puzzle: a boy drew 10 birds and bees in all: their legs together are 48: how many bees and how many birds did the boy draw (a bee has 6 legs and a bird 2 legs: 7 bees and 3 birds); discuss how the sum was found (a. a girl makes flowers with ribbon: if 1.5 m of ribbon goes into making 1 flower, how many metres of ribbon are needed to make 3 flowers (1.5 + 1.5 + 1.5 = 4.5 m, shown by the three hops of 1.5 along the number line from 0 to 6)?; b. depict it by the plate model); find the expressions' values and explain the pattern (the parallelogram cards of expressions whose sums make wholes and tens: 4.8 − 3.8; 8.8 + 1.2; 4.18 + 3.22; 0.8 + 0.2; 3.7 + 3.7; 5.88 + 4.12; 0.3 + 0.7; 7.6 + 2.4; 88.6 − 71.2); a. find the fractions whose sum is 1 and write the equality (□ + □ = 1); b. find the fractions whose sum is greater than 1 and write the inequality (□ + □ > 1) (the cloud of mixed numbers to choose from: 2/3, 0.9, 7/8, 0.8, 1/2, 1/3, 0.1, 0.5, 0.3, 1/8, 0.2); write the whole-number values that make the inequality true (1.5 < □ < 5.3); with the digits 4, 0 and 8, how many decimal fractions can be written?; a regular hexagon's perimeter is 42 dm: find the frame length of the square whose side is equal to its side (the hexagon's side is 7 dm, so the square's perimeter is 28 dm); the value of the sum of two numbers is 10 and the difference is 3: find these numbers (6.5 and 3.5); the sum of two numbers is equal to 2.8 and the value of their difference is 0.2: find the numbers (1.5 and 1.3); make an interesting square and test yourselves (the three 3 × 3 magic squares to complete: with 7.5, 2.5, 0.5, 8.5 placed; with the decimals 151.2, 340.2, 189, 37.8, 302.4; with 19.68, 18.96, 18.24, 19.13) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 112–113 |
| Scored self-test on decimals — test your skill (Чадвараа сориорой) — the scored test (1 point (оноо) for each item unless marked): 1. writing the digits from 1 to 9 in sequence, and putting action signs and brackets between them, make 100 (1 point for each different method); 2. break the number 608008 into the sum of its place addends; 3. when a boy went to school the air temperature was 5°C and when he returned it had become 0°C: by how many degrees did the air temperature fall?; 4. write the decimal fractions in ascending order (809.09; 8900.90; 8080.90; 800.09; 890.900); 5. one sack of flour is 0.5 centners: how many kilograms are two sacks of flour (100 kg)?; 6. write the fractions whose sum is 1 (the four split diagrams of 1: 0.8 and □; 0.3 and □; 0.25 and □; 0.48 and □); 7. how many tenths parts are equal to one whole (10)?; 8. name 5 decimal fractions, with tenth divisions, lying between 5 and 6; 9. express the pictured segments by a common fraction, correctly matched (the three shaded bars, to be matched with the ovals 0.5, 0.2 and 0.75); 10. (2 points) in two boxes there are 12.4 kg of sugar: if 1.2 kg of sugar is taken from the first box and put into the second box, the two boxes will hold equal measures of sugar: how many kilograms of sugar were in each box (7.4 kg and 5.0 kg)?; 11. (2 points) observe the table and write the suitable measure (the centimetre row 8.5, □, 15.2, □, 102, □ against the millimetre row □, 183, □, 900, □, 8); 12. convert the length units (4 m = □ cm; 7 cm = □ m; 76 m = □ cm; 7.6 cm = □ mm); the score table for marking yourself: 12 points and above — "Very good. I wish you success"; 10-12 points — "Good. Keep trying"; 8-9 points — "Try harder"; 7 points and below — "Take help from your friends and your teacher" | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 115 |
| Reference summary: reading, comparing, rounding, operations on decimals — a reference inquiry about the decimal fraction (Аравтын бутархайн тухай лавлах): let's consolidate our knowledge and make a little reference book (Мэдлэгээ бататган, бяцхан лавлах ном бүтээгээрэй): the summary table of reading and writing (the place chart of 432.52 — the whole part's hundreds, tens and ones, the point, the fraction part's tenths and hundredths — with the writing 432 52/100 = 432.52 and the reading "four hundred thirty-two whole fifty-two hundredths"); the summary of comparing (Жиших): Method 1: on the number line, the number on the right-hand side is always the bigger; Method 2: 1. compare the whole parts (3.52 and 3.54); 2. if the whole parts are equal, compare the tenths parts; 3. if the tenths parts are equal, compare the hundredths parts: 3.52 < 3.54; the summary of rounding to a whole: Method 1: round to the whole number lying nearest on the number line; Method 2: if the tenths part's digit is smaller than 5, write the whole part as it is, drop the tenths part's digit and round to a whole (15.3 ≈ 15; 23.45 ≈ 23); if the tenths part's digit is greater than 5 or equal to 5, add 1 to the whole part's digit, drop the tenths part and round to a whole (15.8 ≈ 16; 23.52 ≈ 24); the summary table of the decimal-fraction actions (Аравтын бутархайн үйлдлүүд): adding and subtracting — 1. write the whole and fraction parts' places opposite each other; 2. carry out the action like the addition or subtraction of many-digit numbers; 3. if a place is lacking in the fraction part, write a zero at the end to complete it (the worked examples: 2.46 + 5.73 = 8.19; 20.7 − 4.9 = 15.8); adding mentally — 1. say a decimal fraction whose sum with the given one is 1; 2. say a decimal fraction whose sum is 10; multiplying and dividing by tens (Аравтаар үржүүлэх, хуваах) — when a number is multiplied by 10, 100, 1000, the number's places' values move forward by 1, 2, 3 places in turn; when a number is divided by 10, 100, 1000, the number's places' values move back by 1, 2, 3 places in turn | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 116 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 68 countries (Kazakhstan, Jordan, South Africa, Egypt, Greece, Armenia, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, Libya, Vietnam, Ghana, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, North Macedonia, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Turkey, Tanzania, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Australia, Brazil, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Eritrea, Chile, Finland, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Guyana, Gabon, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Italy, Iceland, Suriname, Papua New Guinea).** Nepal carries decimals from tenths and hundredths with fraction↔decimal conversion in Grade 3 (L23, Nepali pp. 96–99; English 2074 pp. 97–100), through decimal place value and addition/subtraction in Grade 4 (L4 §4.2), ×/÷ by 10/100/1000 and rounding to thousandths in Grade 5 (Unit 12, pp. 77–95), ×/÷ by whole numbers and by decimals in Grade 6 (पाठ 5, pp. 76–85), decimal operations in word problems in Grade 7 (Unit 16, pp. 139–143), to terminating vs repeating decimals with repeating decimals → fractions in Grade 8 (Unit 14, pp. 110–118). The Grade 3 English p. 99 slips '2/100 = 0.002' (correct 0.02) and '2.1 read as two point two one' (correct 'two point one') are taught corrected; the Nepali book has both right. Bangladesh carries decimals from first decimals in Class 4 (Ch. 9, pp. 103–120) — 0.1, the decimal point, tenths/hundredths/thousandths, addition and subtraction, ×/÷ by 10 and 100, decimal ↔ fraction — through decimal fractions to thousandths with ×/÷ by integers, by 10/100 and by decimals in Class 5 (Ch. 7, pp. 65–88), the decimal place-value game with multiplication and division of decimal fractions in Class 6 (Ch. 4, pp. 57–102), and GCD/LCM of decimal fractions in Class 7 (Exp. 3, pp. 57–79), to finite, infinite and recurring decimals in Class 8 (Exp. 2, pp. 25–46). Iraq carries decimals from tenths and hundredths with comparing and converting in Grade 4 (4/5 = 0.8), through decimals to thousandths with comparing, ordering, rounding and converting in Grade 5, to decimal multiplication and division — by whole numbers and by decimals — in Grade 6 (Ch. 4, pp. 74–97). Kazakhstan carries the fullest decimal Iran begins decimals with tenths in Grade 4, takes them to thousandths with three of the four operations in Grade 5, and adds decimal division in Grade 6.
course — reading, converting, comparing, all four operations,
powers of ten and rounding; Jordan carries tenths and hundredths
in Grade 4, thousandths with all four operations in Grade 5, and
decimal multiplication and division in Grade 6; Egypt carries
decimals to thousandths with addition and subtraction in Grade 5
Term 1 — including ordering, rounding and the number line at the
book's printed folios — decimal multiplication in the same term, and decimal
division in Grade 6 (rows completed by the 2026-11-08
re-verification sweep); Greece carries decimal fractions in Grade 3,
three-place decimals in Grade 4, and the full operations in Grade
5; Armenia carries the whole decimal chapter in Grade 6; Sierra
Leone carries tenths decimals in Primary 4 (teacher plans),
hundredths with all four operations in Primary 5 (teacher plans),
and place value with rounding in JSS 1; Mauritania carries
decimals only from Grade 5 — its early grades teach no decimals
at all, stated honestly above; Libya carries tenths, hundredths
and thousandths with all four operations in Grade 4, and ×/÷ by
10, 100, 1000 in Grade 5; Vietnam carries the decimal concept
with comparing and rounding in Grade 5 and decimal computation
in Grade 6; Ghana carries decimals only as teacher-manual
evidence (B4's tenths and decimal addition/subtraction, B6's
comparison by long division); South Africa carries first decimals
as tenths on a metre line in Grade 5, systematic decimals to two
places in Grade 6, and the full decimal worksheets in Grade 7. Laos carries decimals from tenths and hundredths through litres in Grade 4 (to thousandths, with decimal × whole numbers, in the old Grade 4 book) to full decimal calculation, including decimal ÷ decimal, in Grade 5. Yemen carries decimals from tenths and the decimal point in Grade 4, through rounding and all four operations with fraction-to-decimal conversion, to estimating decimal operations in Grade 6. Indonesia carries decimals from tenths and hundredths in Grade 4, through fraction-to-decimal conversion with comparing and ordering (but no decimal operations) in Grade 6, to rational numbers, repeating decimals and decimal operations in Grade 7 Merdeka; Grade 5 teaches no decimals as a topic of their own. Nicaragua carries decimals from tenths with addition and subtraction in Grade 3 (old and 2025 books), through hundredths and thousandths with comparing and rounding in Grade 4, to decimal ×/÷ by naturals in Grade 5 and decimal ×/÷ decimal in Grade 6, revised in the Grade 7 student book and as rational-number decimals in the Grade 7 module (module evidence). Ethiopia carries decimals from tenths and hundredths with comparing in Grade 4, through place value and all four operations in Grade 5, to fraction–decimal conversion, terminating vs non-terminating decimals and operations on decimals in Grade 6, and terminating and repeating decimals in Grades 8–9 (0.777… = 7/9). South Sudan carries decimals from fractions↔decimals in Grade 5 (0.5 → 1/2; 3/4 → 0.75), through decimal conversions in Grade 6, square roots of decimals in Grade 7 (√0.0169 = 0.13), squares and roots of decimals in Grade 8 (0.4² = 0.16; √0.36 = 0.6), to fractions, percentages and decimals with recurring decimals in Secondary 1. Honest limits: Grade 5 sets 1/3 as a decimal without noting it recurs — teach it as 0.333…; Grade 6 prints "1/3 = 0.3", which is wrong (0.333…); Grade 7 prints "√0.0168" for √0.0169. Grade 4 is not cited for decimals — it shows conversion results only, not the topic. Cambodia carries decimals whole from tenths in Grade 4 through thousandths and negative decimals in Grades 6–7. Kuwait carries decimals from tenths and hundredths with decimal addition and subtraction in Grade 4, through whole-number-and-decimal units in Grades 5, 6 and 7, to recurring decimals inside rational numbers in Grade 8. North Macedonia enters from its main set — Grade 4 (2023), Grade 5 (2022), Grade 6 (2023) and Grade 7 (2025).

China carries decimals from their first meeting in Grade 3 to the full Grade 4 treatment — place value, the trailing-zero property, point movement and column arithmetic — with real-number decimal work in Grade 7.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries decimals from Grade 4's tenths, hundredths and thousandths through Class 5's four operations to Classes 7–9's terminating and recurring decimals.

Syria builds decimals in Grade 5 from tenths to thousandths, with comparison by equalising decimal places and the explicit warning that more decimal places does not mean a larger number, then completes the four operations in columns in Grades 5–6 — multiplication both through fractions and by the ignore-the-points rule — and uses decimal arithmetic inside rational operations in Grades 7–8.

Saudi Arabia teaches decimals across two Grade 4 chapters — grids for tenths and hundredths, the warning case 4.07 (not 4.70), number-line ordering, equivalence with fractions, then rounding, estimating and column adding/subtracting — with the older 1445 Term-3 volume witnessing both chapters and the partial exercises book adding practice evidence.

Malaysia treats decimals in Form 1 as the mirror of its fraction work: signed decimals on the number line, ordered, and carried through combined operations.

Rwanda teaches decimals from Year 4's tenths and hundredths — place value, the number line, and adding and subtracting by aligning decimal points — through Year 5's thousandths and fraction ↔ decimal conversion by division, scaling and place value, Year 6's rounding carried to six decimal places and recurring decimals converted by the multiply-and-subtract method, to Senior 1's terminating and recurring decimals with all four operations.

Sudan gives decimals a unit of rare depth in Grade 5 — notation and place value, comparison, all four operations, and conversions both ways, including long-division conversions — then treats decimals as rationals in Grade 7 and recurring decimals, converted to fractions by the multiply-and-subtract method, in Grade 8.

India stocks decimals from Math-Magic Class 5 and Mathematics Class 6–7, 9–10: tenths; tenths: a block split in 10; writing 2.3 and rupees-paise as decimals (₹5.75); place value; decimal expansions of rationals: terminating or non-terminating recurring, by long division.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик IV, V, VI (Grades 4, 5, 6): chapter 3: reading and writing decimal fractions; decimal and common fraction conversions; decimals on the number line; division giving decimal quotients.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Aventuras de Patacalientes* (Grade 3), *Contando con los recursos* (Grade 4), *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5), *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6), *Matemática Para La Vida* (Grade 7), *Conciencia Matemática* (Grade 8) and *La Matemática de la Belleza* (Grade 9). From its books: from Grade 3: tenths from coins — décimas, centésimas and milésimas as places to the right of the unit; the decimal comma; 50 céntimos = 0,50 = 5 décimas; from Grade 6: addition — Adding decimals aligned by place, with the properties restated for decimals (commutative, associative, neutral element 0): 1,25 + 3,52 = 4,77; from Grade 9: which fractions terminate — a fraction in lowest terms gives a finite decimal iff the denominator's prime factors are only 2s and 5s.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 6: decimal fractions and decimal place value; comparing decimals; adding and subtracting decimals; multiplying decimals; multiplying a decimal by 10, 100 and 1000; dividing a decimal by a decimal; dividing a decimal by a whole number; dividing a decimal by 10, 100 and 1000; changing common fractions into decimals; changing decimals into common fractions; recurring decimals; changing recurring decimals into common fractions; from Grade 7: converting rational numbers to decimal numbers.

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Decimal numbers (tenths/hundredths, place value); Operations with decimal numbers (column addition/subtraction). from Grade 6 (teacher's book): : fractions and decimal numbers; equivalence of fractions and decimals (plans 15–16); : addition and subtraction of decimal numbers (plan 30). from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .1 Operations with numbers: written and mental addition/subtraction of decimal numbers; estimating costs;…; .1 Ordering decimal numbers and rounding (plan 48); .3 Calculations with decimal numbers: multiplying/dividing by numbers between 0 and 1 (plan 50); .1 Fractions and decimal numbers (plan 125). from Grade 9 (teacher's book): Recurring decimals; converting fraction ↔ recurring decimal (1/9 = 1.9̇ discussion); terminating vs recurring. from Grade 10 (teacher's book): Full conversion circle fractions ↔ decimals ↔ percentages, incl. recurring decimals to fractions.

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 7: Números decimales; Conversión de fracciones a decimal; Operaciones con decimales; División de decimales; Fracción generatriz; from Grade 8: Operaciones combinadas; from the Compendium's Secundaria 1 section (Grade 7): Números decimales como consecuencia de los racionales.

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Decimal fractions: fractions with denominators 10, 100, 1000… written with a decimal comma; Properties of decimals: appending or dropping zeros at the right, zeros before the…; Decimal place values after the comma (tenths, hundredths, thousandths…); Comparing decimals (integer parts first, then equalising decimal places with zeros); Adding decimals in columns with commas aligned (national-area example totalling 143.1…; Subtracting decimals in columns, checking by addition; Multiplying a decimal by a natural number; Multiplying a decimal by 10, 100, 1000… by moving the comma right; Multiplying a decimal by a decimal (comma placed by total decimal places); Dividing a decimal by a natural number; Dividing a decimal by 10, 100, 1000… by moving the comma left; Dividing a decimal by a decimal (shifting the comma in both numbers); from Grade 6: Converting a decimal to a common fraction and a common fraction to a decimal by long…; Periodic (recurring) decimals: pure and mixed, period notation 0,(7), 0.56(123); from Grade 7: Evaluating numerical expressions with common and decimal fractions (exercise sets); from Grade 8: Every rational number as an infinite periodic decimal, and conversely.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — decimals are named only in its Class 1 strand title, 'Fractions and Decimals', with no decimal objective anywhere at Class 1 or in the Class 2 body; its Class 3 syllabus requires the concept of a decimal, decimal notation read and written up to thousandths, operations with decimals up to thousandths, and the relationship between fractions and decimals; Class 4 maintains the same programme, its body reaching dividing a decimal fraction by a whole number and by a decimal fraction. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 6: place value extended past the units: a whole-number place table (49 181 228 visitors) read as a ×10 / ÷10 pattern and extended one step each way toward tenths; history of decimal notation (Stevin, Kepler, Napier, al-Kashi) used to motivate decimals as fractions; decimal part as a sum of unit fractions with denominators 10, 100, 1000: 2/5 and 1/25 of a 100 m² garden combined; 0,47 = 4/10 + 7/100; place-value table for a decimal: 12,467 expanded digit by digit (tenths 0,4; hundredths 0,06; thousandths 0,007); reading decimals off fraction models and off subdivided number lines (3,8–3,9 in tenths of a step); rounding decimals on the number line (6,72 and 6,77 between 6,7 and 6,8) linked to rounding whole numbers (672 to tens); rule stated: look at the digit right of the target place, 0–4 keep, 5–9 raise; rounding practice to tenths, units and hundredths (1,024; 18,81; 4,445); ordering pupils by height and mass with mixed decimal lengths (1,48; 1,5; 1,50); fraction → decimal by long division of numerator by denominator, taught as a second method beside widening to a power of ten (7/5 = 1,4 both ways, checked on a calculator); converting sets of fractions by division and matching to given decimals (12/5, 81/10, 37/25, 135/50, 6/8, 40/16, 18/90, 21/24); division answers as decimals in sharing problems (cake for 2; 0,2 L per km; square garden of perimeter 17 m → side 4,25 m; 144 TL among 5; 254 m road lighting spacing); terminating vs repeating decimal parts contrasted by dividing 1/4 = 0,25 and 1/3 = 0,333…; a quotient's decimal part is finite or eventually repeating (stated); recurring decimals (devirli ondalık gösterimler) named and notated with the overbar: 0,1666… = 0,16̄, 4,1212… = 4,12̄; fractions that cannot be written with denominator 10, 100, 1000 recur; expanding recurring decimals from overbar form and back (2,5̄ = 2,555…); branching-tree true/false diagnosis of overbar equalities; decimal addition and subtraction by place-value table: align the commas and same places, pad with zeros on the right (3,25 + 2,1 = 5,35; 5,7 − 1,49 = 4,21); rule stated; decimal add/subtract problems: 3,4 kg + 2,56 kg fruit; 1339,90 + 1419,99 TL clothes; 45,67 − 27,74 L petrol; 720 − 599,99 TL discount; strip lengths 2,34 cm and 5,047 cm against a 15 cm pencil; decimal multiplication by three pupil methods compared: repeated addition (3,2 + 3,2 + 3,2), conversion to fractions (32/10 × 3), and ignoring the comma then placing it by total decimal places; decimal multiplication rule stated with worked layout 2,45 × 3,7 = 9,065 (2 + 1 = 3 decimal places in the product); decimal multiplication modelled: 7 × 0,4 = 2,8 on the number line; 0,3 × 0,4 = 0,12 on a hundredths grid; a natural number times a decimal below 1 gives a smaller result (stated); decimal multiplication problems: 0,75 kg at 89,4 TL/kg; a clock losing 1,5 s/hour over 3 days; rectangle area 4,8 × 7,2 m; change from 250 TL for two priced fruits; short multiplication by 10, 100, 1000: comma moves 1, 2, 3 places right, zeros appended when places run out (3,456 × 1000 = 3456; 14,06 × 1000 = 14 060); rule stated; decimal division motivated by bottling: 15 L into 0,5 L bottles and 20 L into 1,25 L bottles, solved by converting decimals to fractions; dividing by a decimal below 1 enlarges a natural number (stated); decimal division two official ways: (1) write both as fractions and divide fractions (3,5 ÷ 0,5 = 7); (2) multiply both by 10/100/1000 to make natural numbers (1,8 ÷ 0,45 = 180 ÷ 45 = 4); hundredths-grid modelling of ÷ 0,15 and ÷ 0,005; decimal division problems: 3,5 L of milk for 157,5 TL → 45 TL/L; 48,4 kg rice in 1,1 kg packets and 66 kg bulgur in 1,2 kg packets, total revenue; short division by 10, 100, 1000: comma moves 1, 2, 3 places left, zeros prepended when needed (537,8 ÷ 1000 = 0,5378; 23 ÷ 1000 = 0,023); rule stated; from Grade 7: Rational numbers in everyday measures first sorted as finite vs recurring decimal representations; Predicting finite vs recurring decimal form from the simplified denominator: no prime factor other than 2 or 5 means finite, otherwise recurring; Converting a fraction to a decimal by rewriting the denominator as a power of 10; Converting a terminating decimal to a fraction a/b; Converting a recurring decimal to a fraction: numerator is the whole written part minus the non-repeating part, denominator is as many 9s as repeating digits and as many 0s as non-repeating decimal digits; The recurring decimal 0.999… equals 1, justified three ways: number-line closeness, conversion to 9/9, and 1/3 × 3.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: decimals built from fractions — tenths and hundredths; decimal place value to thousandths; reading and writing decimals with the digits after the point read one by one; fractions changed to decimals by long division; adding and subtracting decimals with carrying and borrowing; a decimal multiplied by a whole number.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Mathematics 6 (student); the Algebra 8 methodological guide (teacher evidence; it companions a different, unheld Gogyan student book, not the held Mikayelyan book); Algebra and Elements of Mathematical Analysis 10 (Arakelyan); the Grade 10 advanced-programme teacher guide, Semester 1 (teacher evidence): the decimal fraction; decimal fractions smaller than one; zeros at the right of a decimal; picturing decimal fractions on the coordinate ray; adding and subtracting decimal fractions; periodic decimal fraction; converting a periodic decimal fraction to a rational number; periodic decimal fractions; writing rational numbers as decimal fractions.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 31 teacher-evidence items from 11 of the guides (Book 14, Book 18, Book 19, Book 20, Book 21, Book 22, Book 23, Book 24, Book 26, Book 29, Book 30; Grades 4, 5, 6). Decimals enter the guides' programmes from Grade 4.

**Book 14:** *Switch On! 6e année, Guide de l'enseignant* (teacher guide, Grade 6, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 4,181,721 bytes, 88 PDF pages. Items are the distinct drill strands in its Sommaire; confirmation tests excluded in the placements. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 18:** *Switch On! Every Day 6e, livret d'activités rituelles* (Grade 6, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 4,058,172 bytes, 109 PDF pages. Same series and standing as Book 9. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 19:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 5, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 70,552,470 bytes, 330 PDF pages; new edition September 2020, ministry approval serial 515220 (3 Aug 2020). Its contents list 32 numbered lessons in 6 units, read from rendered pages (legacy font encoding). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 20:** *L'Explicite en Maths CM2, Guide pédagogique* (CM2 = Grade 5) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,455,488 bytes, 122 PDF pages. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 5 programme, corresponding topic-for-topic to Book 19. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 21:** *Switch On! 5e année, Guide de l'enseignant* (teacher guide, Grade 5, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,827,643 bytes, 88 PDF pages. Its Sommaire is a scanned table, read from the rendered page; items are its distinct drill strands; confirmation tests excluded in the placements. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 22:** *Le Monde des Maths 5, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 5e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 5, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,773,818 bytes, 256 PDF pages. Items are its 32 numbered lessons, cited by sequence from its Table des matières. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 23:** *Le Monde des Maths 6, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 6e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 6, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,986,682 bytes, 300 PDF pages. Items are its 32 numbered lessons, printed pp. from its Table des matières. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 24:** *L'Explicite en Maths 6e, Guide pédagogique* (Grade 6) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 8,497,260 bytes, 120 PDF pages. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 6 programme, corresponding to Book 23 with the series' own additions (an interest sheet; a powers sheet). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 26:** *Le Monde des Maths 4, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 4e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 4, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 4,953,260 bytes, 190 PDF pages; read in place from its staging copy (never deleted from staging). Items are its 32 numbered lessons — printed pp. from its Table des matières where printed, later units by sequence. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 29:** *L'Explicite en Maths CM1, Guide pédagogique* (CM1 = Grade 4) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 8,896,704 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 4 programme, corresponding to Books 26 and 30. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 30:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 4, Arabic; contents bilingual Arabic/French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 21,206,485 bytes, 357 PDF pages; 2019 edition. Its contents list the same 32 lessons as Books 26 and 29, with bilingual titles, read from rendered pages. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

Morocco's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 32 guides are read and stocked, but no pupil book is in hand.

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 15 items (11 from student books, 4 teacher evidence) from Book S6, Book T5, Book S7, Book T6.

**Book S6:** *Libro de texto, 4.° grado* (student textbook, Grade 4, MINED 2026-tree edition) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); 21,073,518 bytes, 196 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

Into this lesson the placements stock 6 items (4 from pupil books, 2 teacher evidence) from Book S15, Book S21, Book T35, Book T50.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 15 items (4 from pupil books, 11 teacher evidence) from Book S1, Book S4, Book S13, Book S15, Book T8, Book T9, Book T10, Book T11, Book T12, Book T13, Book T14, Book T15, Book T16, Book T38, Book T40.

## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. The two Vamos a Aprender books (Books 26–27) are image-only viewer assemblies with no text layer, read from rendered pages (covers, printed contents pp. 6–7 and body spot-checks — Matemáticas 10 printed p. 110 “Teorema del seno”, Matemáticas 11 printed p. 120 “Derivada de una función en un punto”); printed page = PDF page in the sampled range. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–27 in the placements' order. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Book errors: none material at placement grain — the Secundaria Activa Grade 9 module's contents page misprints two of its own headings (“Captítulo”, “Undiad 2”); the content is unaffected and rows use the corrected unit names. The placements' named exclusions — presentation letters, “how this book works” pages, author/imprint pages, transversal civics/health/environment themes, practice/evaluation apparatus (“Practica más”, “Evaluación del aprendizaje”, answer keys) and the Escuela Nueva “Estándares/Competencias” boxes at each unidad opening (MEN's own curriculum-standard statements; the taught content is placed at the guías themselves) — are stocked nowhere. The Círculos de Aprendizaje integrated books and the Retos para Gigantes Transición weekly guides are NOT in this set (filed as integrado, not matematicas) and are stocked nowhere. Colombia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (27/27) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 13 teacher-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3, Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 7–10) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 355/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); ISBN 978-99920-0-847-2; 1,808,872 bytes, 56 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.
